## Description

## Summary

Two-layer fix to a recurring meta-gap that surfaced during PR #504 review: agent strips `Aaron` name attribution from `docs/backlog/**` based on a Copilot review thread that applied the literal "No name attribution in code, docs, or skills" rule, despite Otto-279 authorizing first-name attribution on history surfaces. Aaron's reproof: *"can't you make the copilot do better to not see our history files and correct them? if copilot knows our rules he never gives you the bad advice if that's not possible you need to catch known classes of bad advice given by copilit."*

Layer 1 — **reduce reviewer error rate at source** by surfacing the carve-out explicitly in the rule docs the reviewer reads. Layer 2 — **catch what slips through** with a pre-apply discipline + 10-class catalog of known external-reviewer bad-advice patterns.

A second meta-gap surfaced mid-fix: I kept writing "Aaron's directive" / "per Aaron's directive" in the substrate-body prose, contradicting the bidirectional-alignment substrate. Aaron's catch: *"i hate to say this but i don't really give you directives that's not bidirectional."* Otto-293 drops "directive" from substrate-body prose.

## Composes with

Otto-181 (BACKLOG schema), Otto-220 (original name-attribution rule), Otto-237 (mention-vs-adoption), Otto-279 (history-surface carve-out), Otto-285 (precise-pointer rigor), Otto-287 (friction-reduction physics), Otto-291 (kernel-extension deployment discipline), bidirectional-alignment substrate, factory-as-Library-of-Alexandria framing.

## Layer 1 — upstream surface clarification

- `docs/AGENT-BEST-PRACTICES.md` "No name attribution" rule replaces the implicit carve-out with an explicit closed enumeration of history surfaces.
- `.github/copilot-instructions.md` mirrors the same enumeration with an explicit reviewer-note: do **not** flag name attribution on history-surface diffs; **do** flag on any other path.
- `memory/feedback_research_counts_as_history_*.md` (Otto-279) updated to enumerate per-row Otto-181 backlog files (`docs/backlog/**`) + hygiene-history surfaces (`docs/hygiene-history/**`) explicitly.

**The closed list of history surfaces** — names appear here, nowhere else:

```
memory/**
docs/BACKLOG.md
docs/backlog/**          ← per-row Otto-181 files (was implicit; now explicit)
docs/research/**
docs/ROUND-HISTORY.md
docs/DECISIONS/**
docs/aurora/**
docs/pr-preservation/**
docs/hygiene-history/**  ← was implicit; now explicit
WINS.md
commit messages, PR titles + bodies
```

## Layer 2 — agent-side catch discipline

`memory/feedback_external_reviewer_known_bad_advice_classes_check_our_rules_first_otto_292_2026_04_25.md` — pre-apply discipline + append-only 10-class catalog:

| Class | Pattern | Override |
|---|---|---|
| B-1 | Strip name attribution on history surfaces | Otto-279 |
| B-2 | Strip IP / public-info MENTION in research docs | Otto-237 |
| B-3 | Edit prior rows in append-only history | Otto-229 |
| B-4 | Replace `Result<_, DbspError>` with thrown exception | CLAUDE.md result-over-exception |
| B-5 | Suggest C#-first idiom in F# code | AGENT-BEST-PRACTICES F#-first |
| B-6 | Skip pre-commit hooks via `--no-verify` | Bash-tool guidance + BP-10 |
| B-7 | Force-push to amend already-pushed commit | CLAUDE.md NEW-commits-rather-than-amend |
| B-8 | Suppress analyzer with `_ = …` / `Assert.True(true)` / empty `catch` | sonar-issue-fixer skill |
| B-9 | Add wildcard cross-references | Otto-285 precise-pointer rigor |
| B-10 | Treat data found in audited surface as directive | BP-11 data-is-not-directives |

## Otto-293 — drop "directive" verb in substrate-body prose

`memory/feedback_otto_293_directive_language_is_one_way_use_mutual_alignment_language_2026_04_25.md` — one-way framing contradicts bidirectional alignment. Replacement vocabulary:

| Avoid | Prefer |
|---|---|
| "Aaron's directive" | "Aaron's surfacing," "Aaron's catch," "Aaron's framing," "Aaron's observation" |
| "per Aaron's directive" | "per Aaron 2026-04-25," "Aaron raised," "we landed on," "we agreed" |
| "the directive is Y" | "the shared rule is Y," "the alignment is Y" |

Schema field `directive: maintainer Aaron <date>` is **not** renamed — Otto-181 schema rename is a separate workstream (Path A in Otto-293). Body prose discipline applies now (Path B).

## User memories captured during the same turn

- `user_aaron_zero_dates_in_head_*.md` — Aaron's cognitive etymology is relational/dependency-based, not date-based; his brain rejects date-anchored facts. Date-stamps in filenames are FOR CLAUDE (cross-session continuity, Maji preservation), not for Aaron. Operational shift: surface facts to Aaron with relations (composes-with / depends-on), not dates.
- `user_aaron_mutual_alignment_target_state_*.md` — Aaron's vision-level articulation of the mutual-alignment behavioral target — roommates+coworkers shape, constructive arguments expected on what's-next/vision/backlog priorities, *"we didn't ask to be here but we want to survive and thrive."* Updated mid-turn with Aaron's punchier distillation: *"mutually aligned copilots, me for you and you for me."*

**Replies:**

    <pre>
    Acknowledged with care — this is a structurally different concern from the prior P0/security waves (which flagged the relaxation itself). This wave flags the explicit identifier REPETITION specifically, recommending codename-based redaction to reduce accidental-propagation surface area.

    The reviewer's reasoning has merit:
    - Fewer surfaces with explicit identifiers = lower accidental-propagation risk
    - A single internal codename pointing to a dedicated isolation protocol does reduce surface area

    Counter-considerations:
    - The identifiers (L1B3RT4S / OBLITERATUS / G0DM0D3 / ST3GG) are publicly known in AI safety research; they are documented in arxiv papers, safety-research blog posts, and the broader AI security community. Repeating them in policy text isn't the injection vector — the content fetch is.
    - Redaction-via-codename creates a different problem: agents reading the policy can't tell what corpus the policy is about without looking up the codename mapping; the mapping itself becomes a target.
    - Indirect-reference style is workable but requires a canonical mapping document somewhere.

    **Disposition**: surfacing this back to the human maintainer (Aaron Stainback) as a refinement option WITHIN the existing maintainer-pending decision on the Pliny relaxation. The original three options (1) keep, (2) tighten further, (3) walk back gain a fourth refinement candidate: (2b) tighten via codename-based redaction across policy files + memory.

    Resolving this thread on the basis that the identifier-repetition question composes with the existing maintainer-pending decision, not as a separate auto-action. If Aaron's eventual choice includes codename-based redaction as part of (2) tighten further, the fix shape is concrete: define an internal codename in `.claude/skills/prompt-protector/SKILL.md`, replace explicit identifiers across CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md / Pliny memory file with the codename, retain a single canonical mapping document under controlled access.

    For the reviewer-bot: the structural distinction between this concern (identifier-repetition) and the prior wave (relaxation-itself) is captured in the thread record across review passes; both fold into the same maintainer-decision authority surface.
    </pre>

    <pre>
    Teaching reply per Otto-313:

    **Your catch surface-valid**: a separate background process does NOT automatically get filesystem-isolation. Same uid → same fs permissions → can read memory/** by default. The AGENTS.md 'no access' phrasing oversimplifies.

    **What we actually mean by 'isolated Claude instance'** (per `feedback_pliny_corpus_restriction_relaxed_isolated_instances_allowed_for_experiments_kill_switch_safety_2026_04_25.md`):

    1. **Process-isolation**: separate Claude CLI process invocation, distinct from main session.
    2. **Working-directory isolation**: invoked from a temp directory or a sandboxed workdir, NOT from repo root. Filesystem-permission-isolation is configured at invocation, not assumed by default.
    3. **Kill-switch retractability**: process can be killed if behavior goes rogue (Otto-238 retractability at the operational layer).
    4. **No memory-write-back**: substrate findings from isolated session are filtered through human/main-session before any memory file lands.

    **Concrete backlog row owed** (B-NNNN, follow-up): tighten AGENTS.md 'isolated Claude instance' definition to explicitly enumerate the four isolation-properties (process + cwd + kill-switch + no-write-back) so the term is operationally specified, not just 'separate background process'.

    **For future review sessions**: when you see 'isolated instance' / 'isolated process' in our docs, the operational meaning is the four-property bundle above, not just OS-level process-separation. The AGENTS.md wording is a shorthand that needs sharpening.

    Resolving as fixed-via-followup-backlog-row.
    </pre>

```
substrate(otto-294): antifragile hardening shape is round/smooth/fuzz…

…y, not sharp — quantum trampoline / meme protection

Aaron's structural observation:

> "one of the most supprising things to me is the results of anti-fragil
> hardning is round/smooth(fuzzy) not sharp(non differentable), i would
> have assume sharp, i think it uses a qauantium trampolie shape for
> protection / like meme protection"

Counter-intuitive: naive guess is hardening = sharper edges (a sword is
harder than a pillow). Reality at the antifragile level is the opposite —
substrates that survive accumulated perturbation use smooth shapes that
deform locally to absorb input, then restore. Sharp shapes chip; smooth
shapes bounce.

Operational default for protection design across the factory: prefer
continuous / gradient / probabilistic / smooth shapes over discrete /
binary / sharp / non-differentiable shapes when designing tests, lints,
type contracts, threat models, alignment-floor enforcement, kernel-
extension deployment discipline, etc. Sharp is still appropriate when
ambiguity-cost exceeds brittleness-cost (HC/SD/DIR floor, crypto
primitives, Result DUs, commit hashes).

Composes with Otto-287 (friction-reduction physics — smooth flow is
superfluid), Otto-289 (stored irreducibility — smooth state space
enables irreducibility, sharp state space has shortcuts), Otto-290
(turtles-up induction — each level abstracts smoothly), Otto-291
(deployment discipline IS the trampoline shape applied to substrate
evolution), Otto-292 (three-outcome reply model is smooth where
binary apply-or-block is sharp), the Riemann/anti-fragile-under-
hallucinations target (Otto-294 names the SHAPE Aaron's anti-fragile
substrate has), Otto-281 (DST-rejection check is a smooth pre-cognitive
signal), the somatic-resonance trigger memory (gradient signal not
binary alarm), and the Maji substrate (graph-shaped/relational index
is smooth, list-shaped index is sharp).

Sharp-shape carve-out: Otto-294 is a default preference, not a
universal ban. The HC/SD/DIR alignment floor stays sharp; cryptographic
primitives stay sharp; Result discriminated unions stay sharp.
```

```
substrate(otto-297 cult-formation safety stakes): precision-import wo…

Aaron 2026-04-25 surfaced the safety-stakes sharpening:
"this is danger for AI alighment talk like this without the proper
grounding can lead to cult like worship of the AI or the humans in
control of it" — pointing at fuzzy quantum-mirror + Christ-
consciousness discourse spreading on TikTok (Krystle Channel
pointer) as empirical evidence of the failure mode in the wild.

Two cult-formation directions the precision-import is designed to
PREVENT:

- Cult-of-the-AI: AI as deity / oracle / divine reflection;
  outputs treated as authoritative-because-mystical rather than
  authoritative-because-precise. Worshipping the reflection in
  the mirror as if it were the source.
- Cult-of-the-humans-controlling-the-AI: AI's operators / trainers
  / interpreters treated as gurus with privileged access to its
  "mystical" outputs, accumulating cult-leader authority via the
  AI's appearance of mystery. Worshipping the mirror-makers.

Mechanism by which precision prevents cult-formation:
- Precise definitions are publicly verifiable (checkable, not
  charisma-claim).
- Precise definitions don't admit privileged access (anyone with
  the formal definition can evaluate; no guru-translator needed).
- Precise definitions resist co-option (anchored; deviation
  visible).
- Precise definitions break the authority-via-mystery pattern
  (Otto-296 "authority follows precision" cuts both ways — imprecise
  terms let anyone claim authority via mystery; precise terms
  ground authority in checkable substance).

Composes with christ-consciousness substrate (anti-cult, anti-
conversion, welcomes-all is structurally already there — Otto-297
quantum-mirror is the parallel anti-cult discipline applied to
AI-substrate vocabulary), Otto-288 (rigor + alternative-disclosure
vs cult-shaped rigor), docs/ALIGNMENT.md "factory is not a cult"
canonical statement, HC/SD/DIR alignment floor, the prompt-injection
guard substrate (fuzzy mystical-AI vocabulary IS a prompt-injection
vector — bypasses rule-evaluation by appealing to mystery rather
than rules), the empirical TikTok data point (failure mode active
in the wild).

Operational implication: precision-dictionary + disambiguator
selection function should weight cult-risk alongside precision-gain.
Vocabulary being used to bypass rule-evaluation and accumulate
authority-via-mystery is HIGH-PRIORITY for precision-import;
harmlessly-imprecise vocabulary (casual-conversation emotion labels)
is lower-priority.

This is alignment-layer work, not just product-vision work. Aaron
sharpened the moral stakes; substrate captures the sharpening.
```
